Section 8.1: Selecting Appropriate Models

Learning target: Students will determine whether a situation is best modeled by a linear, quadratic, or exponential function.

WTC

WTC 1

U8-S1-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · S1-WTC-MODEL · Table + Graph · constructed/open response

Three relationships share the same inputs. Use the table and the graph-family panel to make a first prediction about each model. Describe what changes regularly, predict the next output for each table column, and name what evidence you would check later before trusting the model beyond the shown data.

$x$ABC
0213
1526
28512
3111024
Answer / solution

A appears linear; B appears quadratic (second differences constant); C appears exponential (ratio 2). Next outputs are A=14, B=17, C=48. Any extension should be checked against more data and context constraints.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct evidence from tables, graphs, and contexts to choose a linear, quadratic, or exponential model.

Expected evidence/job: A appears linear; B appears quadratic (second differences constant); C appears exponential (ratio 2). Next outputs are A=14, B=17, C=48. Any extension should be checked against more data and context constraints.

Representation: Table + Graph

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Use the table to choose a linear, quadratic, or exponential model. State the numerical evidence that supports your choice.

$y$13579
Answer / solution

Linear; the first differences are constantly 2.

Expected evidence/job: pattern evidence

Notes Example 2

Choose the most appropriate function family for the table and support the choice with a difference pattern.

$x$-4-3-2-10
$y$1042410
Answer / solution

Quadratic; the second differences are constant (equal to 4 for unit-spaced inputs).

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply rate, second-difference, and ratio patterns to select an appropriate function family.

Expected evidence/job: second differences

Notes Example 4

U8-S1-EX-04 · Notes Example · Intro · S1-EQUATION-FAMILY · Equation · constructed/open response

Classify $y=-3(x+1)^2+4$ as linear, quadratic, or exponential. Name one structural clue in the equation.

Answer / solution

Quadratic; the input is squared in vertex form.

Notes Example 5

U8-S1-EX-05 · Notes Example · Intro · S1-CONTEXT-MODEL · Context · constructed/open response

The area of a circular garden is recorded as its radius changes. Choose a linear, quadratic, or exponential model for area versus radius and name the behavior that supports your choice.

Answer / solution

Quadratic; area is proportional to the square of the radius.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply rate, second-difference, and ratio patterns to select an appropriate function family.

Practice Set 1 6

U8-S1-P1-06 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S1-REVISE-MODEL · Context + Table data · constructed/open response

The points $(0,2),(1,5),(2,8),(3,11)$ suggest a linear model. A new point $(4,19)$ is measured. Is the original linear model still a strong exact model? Explain using the pattern of change.

Answer / solution

No. The first differences were 3, but the new difference is 8, so the expanded data are not exactly linear.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can determine whether a chosen model is reasonable for a situation and its data pattern.

Expected evidence/job: model revision

Representation: Context + Table data

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 7

U8-S1-P1-07 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S1-ERROR-PATTERN · Verbal + Table pattern · constructed/open response

A table has first differences $3,6,9,12$. Another student says the pattern is linear because the differences are increasing regularly. Identify the better model family and the decisive pattern.

Answer / solution

Quadratic; the first differences are not constant, but their differences (the second differences) are constant at 3.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct evidence from tables, graphs, and contexts to choose a linear, quadratic, or exponential model.

Expected evidence/job: misconception diagnosis

Representation: Verbal + Table pattern

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 8

U8-S1-P1-08 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S1-MODEL-REASONABLE · Equation + Context · constructed/open response

A population model is $P(t)=260(1.04)^t$, where $t$ is years after 2022 and the model was fit to recent data. Explain why using the model for $t=75$ requires caution even though the equation produces a value. State a reasonable time-domain limitation.

Answer / solution

The prediction is far beyond the data used to build the model; the constant 4% rate and surrounding population conditions may not remain valid for 75 years.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply rate, second-difference, and ratio patterns to select an appropriate function family.

Expected evidence/job: domain reasonableness

Representation: Equation + Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 18

U8-S1-P1-18 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S1-MODEL-REASONABLE · Equation + Context · constructed/open response

A population-style model is $P(t)=240(1.05)^t$, where $t$ is years after 2020. Explain why using the model for $t=90$ requires caution even though the equation produces a value. State a reasonable domain limitation.

Answer / solution

The model is justified only over a contextually supported time interval; this input is far outside that interval, so the constant rate and surrounding conditions may not remain valid.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can determine whether a chosen model is reasonable for a situation and its data pattern.

Expected evidence/job: domain reasonableness

Representation: Equation + Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 19

U8-S1-P1-19 · Practice Set 1 · Review · S1-REV-COMPARE · Equation + Table data · constructed/open response

Relationship A is $y=2x+1$. Relationship B has values $(0,1),(1,2),(2,4),(3,8)$. Compare the two families and the way each changes.

Answer / solution

A is linear with constant difference/rate 2. B is exponential with constant ratio 2.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear, quadratic, and exponential functions using multiple representations.

Expected evidence/job: compare function families

Representation: Equation + Table data

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 20

U8-S1-P1-20 · Practice Set 1 · Review · S1-REV-FEATURES · Equation · constructed/open response

Compare $y=(x-4)^2+1$ and $y=2(1.5)^x$. Which has a turning point, and which has a constant multiplicative rate?

Answer / solution

The quadratic has a turning point at $(4,1)$. The exponential has constant multiplicative factor 1.5.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear, quadratic, and exponential functions using multiple representations.

Expected evidence/job: feature comparison

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 21

U8-S1-P1-21 · Practice Set 1 · Review · S1-REV-MATCH · Verbal/Table pattern · constructed/open response

Table A has outputs [2, 4, 6, 8]; Table B has outputs [1, 0, 1, 4]; Table C has outputs ['3', '6', '12', '24'] for consecutive integer inputs. Match A, B, and C to linear, quadratic, and exponential families and cite the pattern used.

Answer / solution

A is linear (constant first difference 2); B is quadratic (constant second differences); C is exponential (constant ratio 2).

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare linear, quadratic, and exponential functions using multiple representations.

Expected evidence/job: multiple representation comparison

Representation: Verbal/Table pattern

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 22

U8-S1-P1-22 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery Retrieval · S1-MAS-FORMULA · Equation · constructed/open response

For $2x^2+3x-4=0$, explain why the quadratic formula is a reliable choice and give the exact solutions.

Answer / solution

It does not factor naturally over integers. $x=\frac{-3\pm\sqrt{41}}{4}$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare factoring, completing the square, and the quadratic formula and select efficient solution methods.

Expected evidence/job: method selection and solve

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 23

U8-S1-P1-23 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery Retrieval · S1-MAS-COMPARE · Equation set · constructed/open response

Three equations are $x^2-25=0$, $x^2+10x+7=0$, and $2x^2+3x-5=0$. Name an efficient method for each without solving all three.

Answer / solution

Factoring is efficient for the difference-of-squares equation; completing the square is natural for the monic equation; the quadratic formula is a reliable choice for the third equation.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare factoring, completing the square, and the quadratic formula and select efficient solution methods.

Expected evidence/job: method comparison

Representation: Equation set

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 24

U8-S1-P1-24 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery Retrieval · S1-MAS-METHOD · Equation · constructed/open response

For $x^2-5x+6=0$, choose the most efficient method from factoring, completing the square, or the quadratic formula, then solve.

Answer / solution

Factoring is efficient: $(x-2)(x-3)=0$, so $x=2,3$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare factoring, completing the square, and the quadratic formula and select efficient solution methods.

Expected evidence/job: method selection

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 2 22

U8-S1-P2-22 · Practice Set 2 · Mastery Retrieval · S1-MAS-METHOD · Equation · constructed/open response

For $x^2+x-12=0$, choose the most efficient method from factoring, completing the square, or the quadratic formula, then solve.

Answer / solution

Factoring is efficient: $(x+4)(x-3)=0$, so $x=-4,3$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare factoring, completing the square, and the quadratic formula and select efficient solution methods.

Expected evidence/job: method selection

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 2 23

U8-S1-P2-23 · Practice Set 2 · Mastery Retrieval · S1-MAS-COMPLETE · Equation · constructed/open response

For $x^2+6x-7=0$, explain why completing the square is a reasonable method, then solve.

Answer / solution

Completing the square is direct because the leading coefficient is 1: $(x+3)^2=16$, so $x=1$ or $x=-7$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare factoring, completing the square, and the quadratic formula and select efficient solution methods.

Expected evidence/job: method execution

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 3 23

U8-S1-P3-23 · Practice Set 3 · Mastery Retrieval · S1-MAS-COMPARE · Equation set · constructed/open response

Three equations are $x^2-9=0$, $x^2+8x+3=0$, and $3x^2+2x-5=0$. Name an efficient method for each without solving all three.

Answer / solution

Factoring for $x^2-9=0$; completing the square is natural for $x^2+8x+3=0$; the quadratic formula is reliable for $3x^2+2x-5=0$ (factoring also works if recognized).

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare factoring, completing the square, and the quadratic formula and select efficient solution methods.

Expected evidence/job: method comparison

Representation: Equation set

For $x^2-7x+10=0$, choose the most efficient method from factoring, completing the square, or the quadratic formula, then solve.

Extra Practice 18

U8-S1-PX-18 · Extra Practice · Intro · S1-MODEL-REASONABLE · Equation + Context · constructed/open response

A culture model is $N(t)=40(1.6)^t$ for the first several hours. Explain why a prediction at $t=100$ hours should be treated cautiously.

Answer / solution

The constant growth factor is unlikely to remain valid that long because resources, space, and other conditions will limit growth.

Alignment / evidence

Extra Practice 22

U8-S1-PX-22 · Extra Practice · Mastery Retrieval · S1-MAS-METHOD · Equation · constructed/open response

For $x^2+5x+6=0$, choose the most efficient method from factoring, completing the square, or the quadratic formula, then solve.

Answer / solution

Factoring is efficient: $(x+2)(x+3)=0$, so $x=-2,-3$.

Extra Practice 23

U8-S1-PX-23 · Extra Practice · Mastery Retrieval · S1-MAS-COMPLETE · Equation · constructed/open response

For $x^2-2x-8=0$, explain why completing the square is a reasonable method, then solve.

Answer / solution

With leading coefficient 1, complete the square: $(x-1)^2=9$, giving $x=4$ or $x=-2$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Compare factoring, completing the square, and the quadratic formula and select efficient solution methods.

Expected evidence/job: method execution

Representation: Equation

Section 8.2: Transformations Across Function Families

Learning target: Students will analyze transformations across multiple function families.

WTC

WTC 1

U8-S2-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · S2-WTC-TRANSFORM · Graph · constructed/open response

A parent-function display and transformed-function display are shown. For two different families, track a key point or key feature from parent to image, describe what moved or scaled, and predict how the same transformation notation would affect a third family.

Answer / solution

Use corresponding key points/features to infer the common shifts/scales. The same notation $f(x-h)+k$ shifts every parent right $h$ and up $k$, while the family-specific feature being moved differs.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can build transformed function rules from parent functions across linear, quadratic, exponential, and absolute value families.

Expected evidence/job: Use corresponding key points/features to infer the common shifts/scales. The same notation $f(x-h)+k$ shifts every parent right $h$ and up $k$, while the family-specific feature being moved differs.

Representation: Graph

Exit Ticket A 4

U8-S2-ETA-04 · Exit Ticket A · Intro · S2-PARAM-DESCRIPTION · Equation · constructed/open response

For $f(x)=|x|$, a new function is reflected across the x-axis, vertically stretched by a factor of 2, shifted left 1, and shifted up 4. Build the transformed rule and explain how each parameter produces the stated change.

Answer / solution

$g(x)=-2|x+1|+4$. The negative reflects across the x-axis, 2 gives the vertical stretch, $x+1$ shifts left 1, and $+4$ shifts up 4.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can build transformed function rules from parent functions across linear, quadratic, exponential, and absolute value families.

Expected evidence/job: correct transformed rule connected to parameter effects

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Section 8.3: Absolute Value Functions and Piecewise Thinking

Learning target: Students will analyze and model situations involving absolute value functions.

WTC

WTC 1

U8-S3-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · S3-WTC-DISTANCE · Context + Model · constructed/open response

A courier is at a target location marked 0 on a route. Its signed position changes from negative to positive as it passes the target. Sketch or describe how distance from the target should behave, explain why distance cannot be negative, and propose a rule that turns signed position into distance.

Answer / solution

Distance decreases to 0 at the target and then increases. Absolute value models this: if signed position is $x$, distance is $|x|$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design an absolute value or piecewise representation for situations with distance, change in direction, or two-part behavior.

Expected evidence/job: Distance decreases to 0 at the target and then increases. Absolute value models this: if signed position is $x$, distance is $|x|$.

Representation: Context + Model

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes Example

Notes Example 1

U8-S3-EX-01 · Notes Example · Intro · S3-DISTANCE-MODEL · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A thermostat target is 68°F. Write an expression for the number of degrees a temperature $T$ is away from the target.

Answer / solution

$|T-68|$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design an absolute value or piecewise representation for situations with distance, change in direction, or two-part behavior.

Expected evidence/job: absolute-value distance model

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

U8-S3-EX-02 · Notes Example · Intro · S3-ABS-FEATURES · Equation · constructed/open response

For $y=-2|x+1|+5$, state the vertex and describe the rate of change on each side of the vertex.

Answer / solution

Vertex $(-1,5)$; the left-hand slope is 2 and the right-hand slope is -2.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply key features of absolute value graphs to interpret vertex, intercepts, and rate of change.

Notes Example 4

U8-S3-EX-04 · Notes Example · Intro · S3-PIECEWISE-EVAL · Equation · constructed/open response

For $f(x)=\begin{cases}2x-4,&x\lt 1\\3x+5,&x\ge 1\end{cases}$, find $f(4)$ and identify which rule applies.

Answer / solution

$f(4)=17$; use the second rule because 4 >= 1.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design an absolute value or piecewise representation for situations with distance, change in direction, or two-part behavior.

Expected evidence/job: interval-conditioned output

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes Example 5

U8-S3-EX-05 · Notes Example · Intro · S3-WRITE-PIECEWISE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A delivery fee is 6 dollars plus 2 dollars per mile for the first 5 miles, then 16 dollars plus 1 dollar per mile beyond 5 miles. Write a piecewise cost rule for distance $d\ge0$.

Answer / solution

$C(d)=6+2d$ for $0\le d\le5$, and $C(d)=16+(d-5)$ for $d\gt 5$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply key features of absolute value graphs to interpret vertex, intercepts, and rate of change.

Expected evidence/job: piecewise model

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes Example 6

U8-S3-EX-06 · Notes Example · Intro · S3-DOMAIN-RESTRICTION · Context · constructed/open response

A model gives distance from a trailhead as $d(t)=|2t-10|$ for a 7-hour trip. State a reasonable domain and explain why the algebraic rule should not be used for all real $t$.

Answer / solution

A reasonable domain is $0\le t\le 7$. Times outside the stated trip are not part of the situation.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can predict outputs and intervals from absolute value and piecewise rules in context.

Expected evidence/job: domain restriction

Representation: Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes YTI

Notes YTI 1

U8-S3-YTI-01 · Notes YTI · Intro · S3-DISTANCE-MODEL · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A thermostat target is 72°F. Write an expression for the number of degrees a temperature $T$ is away from the target.

Answer / solution

Notes YTI 5

U8-S3-YTI-05 · Notes YTI · Intro · S3-WRITE-PIECEWISE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A parking garage charges 5 dollars plus 3 dollars per hour for the first 4 hours. After 4 hours, the cost is 17 dollars plus 1.5 dollars for each additional hour. Write a piecewise cost rule for parking time $h\ge0$.

Answer / solution

$C(h)=5+3h$ for $0\le h\le4$, and $C(h)=17+1.5(h-4)$ for $h\gt4$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can apply key features of absolute value graphs to interpret vertex, intercepts, and rate of change.

Expected evidence/job: piecewise model

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

A model gives distance from a trailhead as $d(t)=|5t-20|$ for a 5-hour trip. State a reasonable domain and explain why the algebraic rule should not be used for all real $t$.

Answer / solution

Practice Set 1 8

Explain how $y=2|x-1|$ can be described with two linear rules on either side of $x=1$. Include the slopes and meeting point.

Answer / solution

$y=-2(x-1)$ for $x\lt 1$ and $y=2(x-1)$ for $x\ge 1$; the rays meet at $(1,0)$ with slopes -2 and 2.

Practice Set 1 20

U8-S3-P1-20 · Practice Set 1 · Review · S3-REV-EXP-EQ · Equation · constructed/open response

Explain why $y=7(0.6)^x$ is exponential and whether it represents growth or decay.

Answer / solution

The variable is in the exponent and the constant ratio/base is 0.6; since $0\lt 0.6\lt 1$, it is decay.

Practice Set 2 18

U8-S3-P2-18 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · S3-ABS-PIECEWISE-CONNECT · Multiple representations · constructed/open response

Explain how $y=0.5|x+3|$ can be described with two linear rules on either side of $x=-3$. Include the slopes and meeting point.

Answer / solution

$y=-0.5(x+3)$ for $x\lt -3$ and $y=0.5(x+3)$ for $x\ge -3$; the rays meet at $(-3,0)$ with slopes -0.5 and 0.5.

Alignment / evidence

A shipping service charges 8 dollars plus 2 dollars per pound for packages up to 6 pounds, then 20 dollars plus 1 dollar per pound beyond 6 pounds. Write a piecewise cost rule for weight $w\ge0$.

A model gives distance from a trailhead as $d(t)=|3t-15|$ for an 8-hour trip. State a reasonable domain and explain why the algebraic rule should not be used for all real $t$.

Answer / solution

A reasonable domain is $0\le t\le 8$. Times outside the stated trip are not part of the situation.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can predict outputs and intervals from absolute value and piecewise rules in context.

Expected evidence/job: domain restriction

Representation: Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Extra Practice 16

U8-S3-PX-16 · Extra Practice · Intro · S3-GRAPH-ABS · Graph · constructed/open response

Use the graph to state the vertex and x-intercepts of the absolute-value function. Then identify one interval where outputs decrease.

Answer / solution

Vertex $(-2,-6)$; x-intercepts $(-5,0)$ and $(1,0)$. Outputs decrease for $x\lt-2$ (equivalently on $(-\infty,-2]$).

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design an absolute value or piecewise representation for situations with distance, change in direction, or two-part behavior.

Expected evidence/job: graph features

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Extra Practice 17

U8-S3-PX-17 · Extra Practice · Intro · S3-ABS-EQUATION · Equation + Context · constructed/open response

Find the two numbers that are 4 units from 5 by solving $|x-5|=4$.

Answer / solution

$x=1$ or $x=9$.

Extra Practice 18

U8-S3-PX-18 · Extra Practice · Intro · S3-ABS-PIECEWISE-CONNECT · Multiple representations · constructed/open response

Explain how $y=2|x+4|$ can be described with two linear rules on either side of $x=-4$. Include the slopes and meeting point.

Answer / solution

$y=-2(x+4)$ for $x\lt -4$ and $y=2(x+4)$ for $x\ge -4$; the rays meet at $(-4,0)$ with slopes -2 and 2.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can predict outputs and intervals from absolute value and piecewise rules in context.

Expected evidence/job: absolute value/piecewise connection

Representation: Multiple representations

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Extra Practice 20

U8-S3-PX-20 · Extra Practice · Review · S3-REV-EXP-EQ · Equation · constructed/open response

Explain why $y=2(2.5)^x$ is exponential and whether it represents growth or decay.

Answer / solution

The variable is in the exponent and the base is 2.5; since $2.5\gt1$, it is growth.

A height model factors as $h(t)=-4(t-3)(t-8)$. At what times is the height 0, and which times are meaningful if $t$ is seconds after launch?

The zeros are $t=3$ and $t=8$ seconds; both are nonnegative and meaningful within a modeled interval that includes them.

For $f(x)=\begin{cases}3x+1,&x\lt 2\\-2x+8,&x\ge 2\end{cases}$, find $f(-1)$ and identify which rule applies.

Answer / solution

$f(-1)=-2$; use the first rule because -1 < 2.

CYU 4

U8-S3-CYU-04 · CYU · Intro · S3-WRITE-PIECEWISE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A service fee is 5 dollars plus 3 dollars per unit for the first 4 units, then the rate changes to 1 dollars per additional unit beyond 4. Write a continuous piecewise cost rule for $d\ge0$.

Answer / solution

$C(d)=5+3d$ for $0\le d\le 4$, and $C(d)=17+(d-4)$ for $d\gt 4$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design an absolute value or piecewise representation for situations with distance, change in direction, or two-part behavior.

Expected evidence/job: piecewise model

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Exit Ticket A

Exit Ticket A 1

U8-S3-ETA-01 · Exit Ticket A · Intro · S3-ABS-FEATURES · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A sensor receives a penalty of 3 points for every unit a reading $x$ is away from the target value 50. Design an absolute-value model $P(x)$ for the penalty, then state the vertex and explain its meaning.

Answer / solution

$P(x)=3|x-50|$. The vertex is $(50,0)$, meaning the penalty is 0 at the target reading.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design an absolute value or piecewise representation for situations with distance, change in direction, or two-part behavior.

Expected evidence/job: student-designed absolute-value model plus vertex interpretation

Exit Ticket A 4

U8-S3-ETA-04 · Exit Ticket A · Intro · S3-WRITE-PIECEWISE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A phone plan costs 12 dollars plus 3 dollars per gigabyte for the first 4 GB, then 24 dollars plus 2 dollars per gigabyte beyond 4 GB. Write a piecewise cost rule for usage $g\ge0$.

Answer / solution

$C(g)=12+3g$ for $0\le g\le4$, and $C(g)=24+2(g-4)$ for $g\gt4$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can design an absolute value or piecewise representation for situations with distance, change in direction, or two-part behavior.

Expected evidence/job: piecewise model

Representation: Context + Equation

Section 8.4: Modeling Investigation

Learning target: Students will create and analyze mathematical models using real-world data.

WTC

WTC 1

U8-S4-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · S4-WTC-MODELING · Table + Scatter plot · constructed/open response

A small data set and scatterplot describe a bicycle speed experiment. Choose a first model family, state one assumption, use the model idea to make a prediction inside the observed range, and name one reason you might revise the model after collecting more data.

time (s)distance (m)
01
15
29
313
417
Answer / solution

A roughly linear first model is defensible over the short interval, with an explicit assumption of near-constant average speed. A prediction within the observed range is safer than a far extrapolation; curvature or systematic residuals in new data would motivate revision.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can formulate a mathematical model from real-world data and state the assumptions used.

Expected evidence/job: A roughly linear first model is defensible over the short interval, with an explicit assumption of near-constant average speed. A prediction within the observed range is safer than a far extrapolation; curvature or systematic residuals in new data would motivate revision.

Representation: Table + Scatter plot

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

U8-S4-EX-03 · Notes Example · Intro · S4-PREDICT · Equation + Context · constructed/open response

A bike-rental model is $C(h)=8+5h$ for $0\le h\le8$. Predict the cost for 6 hours and interpret the result.

Answer / solution

Notes Example 4

U8-S4-EX-04 · Notes Example · Intro · S4-DOMAIN · Context · constructed/open response

A fitted model describes battery performance for days 0 through 40. Explain why a prediction at day 300 should be treated cautiously even if the formula can be evaluated. Name one condition that might change outside the observed window.

Answer / solution

Day 300 is far outside the observed/justified domain, so the model assumptions may no longer hold; temperature and aging conditions could change.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can formulate a mathematical model from real-world data and state the assumptions used.

Expected evidence/job: domain limitation

Representation: Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes Example 5

U8-S4-EX-05 · Notes Example · Intro · S4-ASSUMPTION · Context · constructed/open response

A linear model predicts distance traveled over a short drive. State one assumption needed for a constant-rate linear model to remain reasonable, and describe what a violation would look like in the data.

Answer / solution

One defensible assumption is that the speed remains approximately constant. If it fails, the first differences/rates would stop being approximately constant.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can extend a model to make predictions within a reasonable domain.

Expected evidence/job: model assumption

Representation: Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes Example 6

U8-S4-EX-06 · Notes Example · Intro · S4-RESIDUAL · Equation + Data point · constructed/open response

A model predicts $y=2x+1$. For the observed point $(4,10)$, compute observed minus predicted and say whether the model underpredicts or overpredicts.

Answer / solution

Predicted value is 9, so observed minus predicted is $10-9=1$. The model underpredicts by 1.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can critique how well a model fits data, constraints, and the situation being studied.

Expected evidence/job: residual-style observation

Representation: Equation + Data point

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes YTI 1

$x$-2-1012

Notes YTI 3

U8-S4-YTI-03 · Notes YTI · Intro · S4-PREDICT · Equation + Context · constructed/open response

A kayak-rental model is $C(h)=10+4h$ for $0\le h\le9$. Predict the cost for 7 hours and interpret the result.

Answer / solution

$C(7)=38$; the model predicts a 38-dollar rental cost for 7 hours.

Notes YTI 4

U8-S4-YTI-04 · Notes YTI · Intro · S4-DOMAIN · Context · constructed/open response

A fitted model describes ticket sales for days 0 through 14. Explain why a prediction at day 120 should be treated cautiously even if the formula can be evaluated. Name one condition that might change outside the observed window.

Answer / solution

Day 120 is far outside the observed/justified domain, so the model assumptions may no longer hold; demand and event timing could change.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can formulate a mathematical model from real-world data and state the assumptions used.

Expected evidence/job: domain limitation

Notes YTI 5

U8-S4-YTI-05 · Notes YTI · Intro · S4-ASSUMPTION · Context · constructed/open response

A linear model predicts water added to a pool. State one assumption needed for a constant-rate linear model to remain reasonable, and describe what a violation would look like in the data.

Answer / solution

One defensible assumption is that the fill rate remains approximately constant. If it fails, the first differences/rates would stop being approximately constant.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can extend a model to make predictions within a reasonable domain.

Expected evidence/job: model assumption

Representation: Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes YTI 6

U8-S4-YTI-06 · Notes YTI · Intro · S4-RESIDUAL · Equation + Data point · constructed/open response

A model predicts $y=3x-1$. For the observed point $(5,16)$, compute observed minus predicted and say whether the model underpredicts or overpredicts.

Answer / solution

Predicted value is 14, so observed minus predicted is $16-14=2$. The model underpredicts by 2.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can critique how well a model fits data, constraints, and the situation being studied.

Expected evidence/job: residual-style observation

Representation: Equation + Data point

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 6

U8-S4-P1-06 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S4-COMPARE-MODELS · Context · constructed/open response

For data that increase by about 10% each equal time interval, compare a linear model and an exponential model. Which better matches the stated change pattern and why?

Answer / solution

Exponential, because repeated percentage change is multiplicative; a linear model assumes constant additive change.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can critique how well a model fits data, constraints, and the situation being studied.

Expected evidence/job: compare plausible models

Representation: Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 7

U8-S4-P1-07 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S4-REVISE · Context + Data · constructed/open response

A linear model fits measurements for $x=0$ through $5$, but later points bend upward with increasing first differences. What model revision should be investigated?

Answer / solution

Investigate a nonlinear model such as quadratic (if second differences stabilize) or exponential (if ratios stabilize), using the new data to decide.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can formulate a mathematical model from real-world data and state the assumptions used.

Expected evidence/job: revise model

Representation: Context + Data

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

250 is the starting amount; 1.04 means the amount is multiplied by 1.04 each time unit, a 4% increase per unit.

Practice Set 1 24

U8-S4-P1-24 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery Retrieval · S4-MAS-QUAD-MODEL · Context · constructed/open response

A rectangular garden has width $x$ and length $24-x$. Write a quadratic area model and state a reasonable domain.

Answer / solution

$A(x)=x(24-x)=-x^2+24x$ with $0\lt x\lt 24$.

Practice Set 2 5

U8-S4-P2-05 · Practice Set 2 · Intro · S4-RESIDUAL · Equation + Data point · constructed/open response

A model predicts $y=2x+3$. For the observed point $(6,14)$, compute observed minus predicted and say whether the model underpredicts or overpredicts.

Answer / solution

The predicted value is $2(6)+3=15$, so observed minus predicted is $14-15=-1$. The model overpredicts by 1.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can extend a model to make predictions within a reasonable domain.

A linear model predicts packages processed over a short shift. State one assumption needed for a constant-rate linear model to remain reasonable, and describe what a violation would look like in the data.

Answer / solution

One defensible assumption is that the processing rate remains approximately constant. If it fails, the first differences or rates would stop being approximately constant.

Answer / solution

Predicted value is 14, so observed minus predicted is $15-14=1$. The model underpredicts by 1.

Section 8.5: Function Synthesis and Capstone Analysis

Learning target: Students will synthesize understanding of function families and representations to solve complex problems.

WTC

WTC 1

U8-S5-WTC-001 · WTC · Intro · S5-WTC-SYNTHESIS · Graph + Strategy · constructed/open response

A function-analysis dashboard shows several familiar family graphs. Imagine that a problem asks you to identify the family, estimate a key feature, and make an exact calculation. Decide which representation you would use first for each job and explain how you would check that all representations describe the same relationship.

Answer / solution

Use the graph for family/shape and approximate key features, an equation for exact algebraic calculation, and a table for exact sampled values/patterns. Cross-check shared points/features so the representations are consistent.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a solution pathway that connects equations, graphs, tables, and contexts across function families.

Expected evidence/job: Use the graph for family/shape and approximate key features, an equation for exact algebraic calculation, and a table for exact sampled values/patterns. Cross-check shared points/features so the representations are consistent.

Representation: Graph + Strategy

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes Example

Notes Example 1

U8-S5-EX-01 · Notes Example · Intro · S5-CHOOSE-REP · Concept · constructed/open response

You need the exact output already listed for a particular input. Which representation—equation, graph, or table—is usually the most direct starting point, and why?

Answer / solution

A table is usually most direct because it displays listed input-output pairs explicitly.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a solution pathway that connects equations, graphs, tables, and contexts across function families.

Expected evidence/job: strategic representation choice

Representation: Concept

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes Example 3

U8-S5-EX-03 · Notes Example · Intro · S5-COMPETE-MODELS · Context · constructed/open response

Two models fit early data: a linear model adds 120 each period; an exponential model grows 8% each period. What additional evidence would best help choose between them?

Answer / solution

Examine later equally spaced data and compare additive differences with multiplicative ratios or residuals from the two candidate models.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess competing solution strategies and choose the representation that best supports the conclusion.

Expected evidence/job: assess competing models

Representation: Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Notes Example 4

U8-S5-EX-04 · Notes Example · Intro · S5-CONFLICT · Multiple representations · constructed/open response

A table has outputs [5, 8, 11, 14] for consecutive integer inputs, while a proposed equation is $y=4(1.2)^x$. Explain the conflict and identify what should be rechecked.

Answer / solution

The table has constant first differences, so it is linear, while the proposed equation is exponential. Recheck whether the equation and table describe the same relationship.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a solution pathway that connects equations, graphs, tables, and contexts across function families.

Expected evidence/job: representation consistency

Representation: Multiple representations

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 1

U8-S5-P1-01 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-CONNECT-TABLE-EQ · Table + Equation · constructed/open response

Use the table to choose a function family and write one matching equation.

$x$0123
$y$261854
Answer / solution

Exponential; each output is multiplied by 4. One matching equation is $y=3(4)^x$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a solution pathway that connects equations, graphs, tables, and contexts across function families.

Expected evidence/job: connect table/equation

Representation: Table + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 2

U8-S5-P1-02 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-COMPETE-MODELS · Context · constructed/open response

Two models fit early data: a linear model adds 30 each period; an exponential model grows 12% each period. What additional evidence would best help choose between them?

Answer / solution

Examine later equally spaced data and compare additive differences with multiplicative ratios or residuals from the two candidate models.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use multiple representations to solve complex function problems and interpret results.

Expected evidence/job: assess competing models

Representation: Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 3

U8-S5-P1-03 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-CONFLICT · Multiple representations · constructed/open response

A table has outputs [18, 14, 10, 6] for consecutive integer inputs, while a proposed equation is $y=9(0.75)^x$. Explain the conflict and identify what should be rechecked.

Answer / solution

The table has constant first differences, so it is linear, while the proposed equation is exponential. Recheck whether the equation and table describe the same relationship.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess competing solution strategies and choose the representation that best supports the conclusion.

Expected evidence/job: representation consistency

Representation: Multiple representations

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 4

U8-S5-P1-04 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-CAPSTONE-FIT · Equation + Table data + Context · constructed/open response

A venue models attendance by $A(t)=200+25t$ for the first several weeks. Actual attendance is [200, 228, 247, 281]. Use both representations to judge whether the linear model is exact or approximate.

Answer / solution

The model is approximate, not exact: predictions are [200, 225, 250, 275], which are close to but not identical to the observed values.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a solution pathway that connects equations, graphs, tables, and contexts across function families.

Expected evidence/job: multi-representation synthesis

Representation: Equation + Table data + Context

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 5

U8-S5-P1-05 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-STRATEGY · Concept · constructed/open response

A problem asks for end behavior, a vertex, and exact solutions. Propose an efficient order for using graph, equation, table, and/or context representations.

Answer / solution

One efficient pathway is graph for end behavior and vertex, then equation methods for exact solutions; other justified orders are possible.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use multiple representations to solve complex function problems and interpret results.

Expected evidence/job: solution pathway

Representation: Concept

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 6

U8-S5-P1-06 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-TRANSLATE · Equation + Table · constructed/open response

For $y=3|x+2|-1$, create three table values centered at the vertex and explain what the table reveals about symmetry. State the line of symmetry your table supports.

Answer / solution

For example $x=-3,-2,-1$ gives $y=2,-1,2$; equal distances from $x=-2$ give equal outputs, showing symmetry about $x=-2$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess competing solution strategies and choose the representation that best supports the conclusion.

Expected evidence/job: translate representations

Representation: Equation + Table

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 7

U8-S5-P1-07 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-MODEL-PREDICT · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A quantity doubles every 4 hours from an initial value of 30. Choose a function family, write a model, and predict the value after 8 hours.

Answer / solution

Exponential; $Q(t)=30(2)^{t/4}$, so $Q(8)=120$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a solution pathway that connects equations, graphs, tables, and contexts across function families.

Expected evidence/job: model choice and use

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 8

U8-S5-P1-08 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-EVIDENCE-ORGANIZER · Multiple representations · constructed/open response

When deciding between linear and exponential, name one useful piece of evidence you could collect from each of an equation, graph, table, and context before making a final conclusion.

Answer / solution

Equation: structure/parameters; graph: shape/key features; table: differences/ratios or exact values; context: quantities, constraints, and reasonable domain.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can use multiple representations to solve complex function problems and interpret results.

Expected evidence/job: evidence synthesis

Representation: Multiple representations

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 9

U8-S5-P1-09 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-GRAPH-SYNTHESIS · Graph · constructed/open response

The panel shows linear, quadratic, exponential, and absolute-value graphs. Choose two panels and explain one feature that distinguishes their families.

Answer / solution

Any correct pair with a valid distinguishing feature, such as constant slope for linear versus a turning point for quadratic, or V-shape for absolute value versus multiplicative growth for exponential.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can assess competing solution strategies and choose the representation that best supports the conclusion.

Expected evidence/job: graph synthesis

Representation: Graph

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 10

U8-S5-P1-10 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-CHOOSE-REP · Concept · constructed/open response

You need the exact value at an input that is not already tabulated. Which representation—equation, graph, or table—is usually the most direct starting point, and why?

Answer / solution

An equation is usually most direct because you can substitute the input and calculate an exact value.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: I can construct a solution pathway that connects equations, graphs, tables, and contexts across function families.

Expected evidence/job: strategic representation choice

Representation: Concept

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 16

U8-S5-P1-16 · Practice Set 1 · Intro · S5-TRANSLATE · Equation + Table · constructed/open response

For $y=2|x+3|+4$, create three table values centered at the vertex and explain what the table reveals about symmetry.

Answer / solution

Using $x=-4,-3,-2$ gives $y=6,4,6$; equal horizontal distances from $x=-3$ give equal outputs, showing symmetry about $x=-3$.

Practice Set 1 22

U8-S5-P1-22 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery Retrieval · S5-MAS-QF · Equation · constructed/open response

Solve $2x^2-3x-4=0$ with the quadratic formula.

Answer / solution

$x=\frac{3\pm\sqrt{41}}{4}$.

Exact target: Solve quadratic equations using the quadratic formula and interpret solutions.

Practice Set 1 23

U8-S5-P1-23 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery Retrieval · S5-MAS-QF-CONTEXT · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A model reaches ground level when $-5t^2+18t+2=0$. Use the quadratic formula and identify the nonnegative solution as the meaningful time.

Answer / solution

$t=\frac{-18\pm\sqrt{364}}{-10}=\frac{18\mp\sqrt{364}}{10}$; the positive root is approximately $3.71$ seconds.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Solve quadratic equations using the quadratic formula and interpret solutions.

Expected evidence/job: formula + interpretation

Representation: Context + Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

Practice Set 1 24

U8-S5-P1-24 · Practice Set 1 · Mastery Retrieval · S5-MAS-DISCRIM · Equation · constructed/open response

Without fully solving, use the discriminant to predict the number of real solutions of $3x^2+2x+5=0$.

Answer / solution

$b^2-4ac=4-60=-56\lt 0$, so there are no real solutions.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Solve quadratic equations using the quadratic formula and interpret solutions.

Expected evidence/job: discriminant interpretation

Representation: Equation

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan + current Algebra Framework + curated section enrichment

$t$ (weeks)0123
Observed total100119141164
Answer / solution

The linear model is closer over weeks 1-3: it predicts 120, 140, and 160, while the exponential model is lower by increasingly larger amounts. Using $L$, week 4 is predicted to be $L(4)=180$, so about 180 total units are expected if the near-linear pattern continues.

Choose an efficient method for each equation and defend each choice in one sentence: $x^2-16=0$, $x^2+8x+1=0$, and $2x^2+3x-7=0$.

Answer / solution

Secure Summative V1 8

U8-SUM-V1-Q08 · Secure Summative V1 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-QUAD-MODEL · Context + Equation + Graph · constructed/open response

A ball height is modeled by $h(t)=-5(t-2)^2+20$ for $0\le t\le4$. State the maximum height and when it occurs, then solve for the times when $h(t)=0$ and interpret them in context.

Answer / solution

Maximum height 20 at $t=2$. Solving $-5(t-2)^2+20=0$ gives $(t-2)^2=4$, so $t=0$ and $t=4$; these are the modeled ground-level times.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Build and interpret quadratic models, including maximums, minimums, intercepts, and contextually meaningful solutions.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U8-MG05: Build and interpret quadratic models, including maximums, minimums, intercepts, and contextually meaningful solutions.

Representation: Context + Equation + Graph

Source basis: Original Unit 8 Assessment Plan — Summative Evidence Plan + current Algebra Framework

Secure Summative V2 3

U8-SUM-V2-Q03 · Secure Summative V2 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-METHOD-SELECT · Equation set + Written reasoning · constructed/open response

Choose an efficient method for each equation and defend each choice in one sentence: $x^2-25=0$, $x^2+10x+3=0$, and $2x^2+5x-9=0$.

Answer / solution

Efficient choices include factoring/difference of squares for $x^2-25=0$, completing the square for the monic equation with even linear coefficient $x^2+10x+3=0$, and the quadratic formula for $2x^2+5x-9=0$ because it does not factor naturally over integers. Correct justified alternatives are acceptable.

Secure Summative V2 4

U8-SUM-V2-Q04 · Secure Summative V2 · Mastery/Summative · SUM-QUAD-MODEL · Context + Equation + Graph · constructed/open response

A ball height is modeled by $h(t)=-4(t-3)^2+36$ for $0\le t\le6$. State the maximum height and when it occurs, then solve for the times when $h(t)=0$ and interpret them in context.

Answer / solution

The maximum height is 36 at $t=3$. Solving $-4(t-3)^2+36=0$ gives $(t-3)^2=9$, so $t=0$ and $t=6$; these are the modeled ground-level times at launch and landing.

Choose an efficient method for each equation and defend each choice in one sentence: $x^2-49=0$, $x^2+6x-2=0$, and $3x^2+x-5=0$.

Answer / solution

Efficient choices include factoring/difference of squares for $x^2-49=0$, completing the square for $x^2+6x-2=0$, and the quadratic formula for $3x^2+x-5=0$ because it does not factor naturally over integers. Correct justified alternatives are acceptable.

Secure Summative V6 8

U8-SUM-V6-Q08 · Secure Summative V6 · Mastery/Summative · ALT6-MODEL-REVENUE · Context + Equation · constructed/open response

A revenue model is $R(p)=-20(p-15)^2+4500$, where $p$ is price in dollars. Interpret the vertex, then find the prices where the model predicts revenue $R=4000$.

Answer / solution

Vertex $(15,4500)$ means the maximum modeled revenue is 4500 dollars at a price of 15 dollars. Solve $-20(p-15)^2+4500=4000$: $(p-15)^2=25$, so $p=10$ or $20$.

Alignment / evidence

Exact target: Build and interpret quadratic models, including maximums, minimums, intercepts, and contextually meaningful solutions.

Expected evidence/job: Secure evidence for U8-MG05: Build and interpret quadratic models, including maximums, minimums, intercepts, and contextually meaningful solutions.
